Mathlib.CategoryTheory.Grothendieck

CategoryTheory.GrothendieckstructureMathlib.CategoryTheory.Grothendieck, type
Wording
no wording
English
Let $F$ be a functor of $C$ and the set of categories. Then the elements of $\operatorname{Grothendieck}$ applied to $F$ are a basic type.
Formula
$F \in \operatorname{Functor} (C , \operatorname{Cat})\vdash \operatorname{Grothendieck} (F)\ \mathsf{set}$
CategoryTheory.Grothendieck.HomstructureMathlib.CategoryTheory.Grothendieck, type
Wording
homomorphism of X and Y (from the name)
English
Let $X$ and $Y$ be elements of $\operatorname{Grothendieck}$ applied to $F$. Then the homomorphisms of $X$ and $Y$ are a basic type.
Formula
$X , Y \in \operatorname{Grothendieck} (F)\vdash \operatorname{Hom} (X , Y)\ \mathsf{set}$
CategoryTheory.Grothendieck.Hom.basedefMathlib.CategoryTheory.Grothendieck, function or constant
Wording
the base of self (from the name)
English
Let $\mathrm{self}$ be a homomorphism of $X$ and $Y$. Then the base of $\mathrm{self}$ is a homomorphism of the base of $X$ and the base of $Y$ defined as $\operatorname{proj0}$ applied to $\mathrm{self}$.
Formula
$\mathrm{self} \in \operatorname{Hom} (X , Y)\vdash \operatorname{base} (\mathrm{self}):= \operatorname{proj0} (\mathrm{self})\in \operatorname{Hom} (\operatorname{base} (X), \operatorname{base} (Y))$
CategoryTheory.Grothendieck.Hom.fiberdefMathlib.CategoryTheory.Grothendieck, function or constant
Wording
the fiber of self (from the name)
English
Let $\mathrm{self}$ be a homomorphism of $X$ and $Y$. Then the fiber of $\mathrm{self}$ is a homomorphism of the action of the functor of the map of $F$ and the base of $\mathrm{self}$ and the object in the fiber of the base object of $X$ and the object in the fiber of the base object of $Y$ defined as $\operatorname{proj1}$ applied to $\mathrm{self}$.
Formula
$\mathrm{self} \in \operatorname{Hom} (X , Y)\vdash \operatorname{fiber} (\mathrm{self}):= \operatorname{proj1} (\mathrm{self})\in \operatorname{Hom} (\operatorname{obj} (\operatorname{toFunctor} (\operatorname{map} (F , \operatorname{base} (\mathrm{self}))), \operatorname{fiber} (X)), \operatorname{fiber} (Y))$
CategoryTheory.Grothendieck.basedefMathlib.CategoryTheory.Grothendieck, function or constant
Wording
the base of self (from the name)
English
Let $\mathrm{self}$ be an element of $\operatorname{Grothendieck}$ applied to $F$. Then the base of $\mathrm{self}$ is an element of $C$ defined as $\operatorname{proj0}$ applied to $\mathrm{self}$.
Formula

CategoryTheory.Grothendieck.compdefMathlib.CategoryTheory.Grothendieck, function or constant
Wording
the composition of f and g (from the name)
English
Let $f$ be a homomorphism of $X$ and $Y$. Let $g$ be a homomorphism of $Y$ and $Z$. Then the composition of $f$ and $g$ is a homomorphism of $X$ and $Z$.
Formula
$f \in \operatorname{Hom} (X , Y), g \in \operatorname{Hom} (Y , Z)\vdash \operatorname{comp} (f , g)\in \operatorname{Hom} (X , Z)$

Let $x_{2}$ be an isomorphism of the action of the functor of the map of $F$ and the homomorphism of $x_{1}$ and the object in the fiber of the base object of $X$ and the object in the fiber of the base object of $Y$. Let $C$ and $D$ be sets. Then the left inclusion of $C$ and $D$ is an element of $\operatorname{FullyFaithful}$ applied to the left inclusion of $C$ and $D$.
Formula
$C , D \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \operatorname{inclLeftFullyFaithful} (C , D)\in \operatorname{FullyFaithful} (\operatorname{inclLeft} (C , D))$
CategoryTheory.Join.inclRightdefMathlib.CategoryTheory.Join.Basic, function or constant
Wording
the right inclusion of C and D (from the name)
English
Let $C$ and $D$ be sets. Then the right inclusion of $C$ and $D$ is a functor of $D$ and the set of joins of $C$ and $D$.
Formula
$C , D \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \operatorname{inclRight} (C , D)\in \operatorname{Functor} (D , \operatorname{Join} (C , D))$

Wording
the isomorphism functor of F (from the name)
English
Let $F$ be a functor of the set of joins of $C$ and $D$ and $E$. Then the isomorphism functor of $F$ is an isomorphism of $F$ and the functor of the composition of the right inclusion of $C$ and $D$ and $F$ and the right whisker of the edge transform of $C$ and $D$ and $F$.
Formula
$F \in \operatorname{Functor} (\operatorname{Join} (C , D), E)\vdash \operatorname{isoMkFunctor} (F)\in \operatorname{Iso} (F , \operatorname{mkFunctor} (\operatorname{comp} (\operatorname{inclLeft} (C , D), F), \operatorname{comp} (\operatorname{inclRight} (C , D), F), \operatorname{whiskerRight} (\operatorname{edgeTransform} (C , D), F)))$

no wording
English
Let $F$ be a functor of $J$ and $C$. Then $\operatorname{toOver}$ applied to $F$ is a cocone of the composition of the costructured arrow of $F$ and $\operatorname{toOver}$ applied to $F$ and the colimit of $F$.
Formula
$F \in \operatorname{Functor} (J , C)\vdash \operatorname{toOver} (F)\in \operatorname{Cocone} (\operatorname{comp} (\operatorname{toCostructuredArrow} (F), \operatorname{toOver} (F , \operatorname{colimit} (F))))$
CategoryTheory.Limits.limit.toStructuredArrowdefMathlib.CategoryTheory.Limits.ConeCategory, function or constant
Wording
the structured arrow of F (from the name)
English
Let $F$ be a functor of $J$ and $C$. Then the structured arrow of $F$ is a functor of $J$ and the set of structured arrows of the limit of $F$ and $F$.
Formula
$F \in \operatorname{Functor} (J , C)\vdash \operatorname{toStructuredArrow} (F)\in \operatorname{Functor} (J , \operatorname{StructuredArrow} (\operatorname{limit} (F), F))$
CategoryTheory.Limits.limit.toUnderdefMathlib.CategoryTheory.Limits.ConeCategory, function or constant
Wording
no wording
English
Let $F$ be a functor of $J$ and $C$. Then $\operatorname{toUnder}$ applied to $F$ is a cone of the composition of the structured arrow of $F$ and $\operatorname{toUnder}$ applied to the limit of $F$ and $F$.
Formula
$F \in \operatorname{Functor} (J , C)\vdash \operatorname{toUnder} (F)\in \operatorname{Cone} (\operatorname{comp} (\operatorname{toStructuredArrow} (F), \operatorname{toUnder} (\operatorname{limit} (F), F)))$
